# The Sorcerer's Cave — Engine Specification (v2)

> **Status:** Living specification of the game-logic engine (`packages/engine`), regenerated 2026-07-01 from the code and its git history.
> **Source of truth:** the engine **code** is authoritative. Where this document and the code disagree, the code wins — treat the discrepancy as a spec bug.
> **Scope:** the pure, deterministic game rules in `packages/engine/src` — data model, RNG, turn lifecycle, movement, chambers, encounters, fights, special areas, artifacts, scoring, and the multiplayer seat model. The React/Three.js client, the Convex backend, and logical UX (v1 §2/§13) are **out of scope** here.
> **Supersedes:** `docs/specs/design-spec.html` (v1), whose §15 "Gap Analysis" and §16 "Implementation Sketches for the Open Gaps" are obsolete — nearly all those gaps are now implemented. See **Appendix B** for the corrections.

## How to read this document

- **Part I — Normative Requirements** is the testable contract: one rule per row, each with a stable **ID**, the authoritative **`file:line`**, and the **test** that pins it (`—` = no direct test; consolidated in Appendix C).
- **Part II — Rules Narrative** is the readable rulebook, cross-referencing requirement IDs in parentheses, e.g. (SC-8.3-5).
- **Requirement IDs** are `SC-<§>-<n>`, stable across edits and aligned with the code's `spec §N` comments. `§MP` = multiplayer.
- **Appendices:** A — data tables & constants (incl. the RNG algorithm and the full state shape); B — corrections vs the v1 spec; C — test-coverage gaps; D — cross-implementation conformance vectors (for ports).

### Section map

| § | Domain | Primary code |
|---|--------|--------------|
| 3 | Core data model | `state.ts`, `data/*`, `decode.ts`, `coords.ts` |
| 4 | Turn lifecycle & action/event contract | `reduce.ts`, `actions.ts`, `selectors.ts` |

| 6 | Map, movement & levels | `map.ts`, `coords.ts`, `decode.ts` |
| 7 | Chambers, draws, hazards & pickup | `chamber.ts`, `hazards.ts`, `pickup.ts` |
| 8 | Stranger encounters | `reaction.ts`, `reduce.ts` |
| 9 | Fights | `combat.ts`, `combatPlan.ts`, `reduce.ts` |
| 10 | Special areas | `special.ts`, `reduce.ts` |
| 11 | Artifacts & treasure effects | `effects.ts`, `reduce.ts`, `selectors.ts` |

| EXT | Extension kit | `state.ts`, `setup.ts`, `decks.ts`, `decode.ts`, `data/creatures.ts`, `data/treasures.ts`, `data/hazards.ts`, `data/areaCards.ts`, `data/smallPack.ts` |

# Part I — Normative Requirements

## §3 Core Data Model

| ID | Requirement | Code | Test |
|----|-------------|------|------|
| SC-3-1 | The engine MUST define exactly 61 area cards (`AREA_CARDS`) in normative index order, from which the Gateway (index 21, value 175) is removed to leave a 60-card large pack. | areaCards.ts:21, areaCards.ts:18 | data.test.ts › has 61 area cards and the Gateway (value 175) at index 21 |
| SC-3-2 | Each area-card value MUST be a bit-field decoded as: bit0(1)=N exit, bit1(2)=E, bit2(4)=S, bit3(8)=W, bit4(16)=chamber, bit5(32)=stairUp, bit6(64)=stairDown, and special = (value>>7)&7 (0 none,1 Gateway,2 Deep Pool,3 Viper Pit,4 Tomb,5 Great Hall). | decode.ts:17-28 | decode.test.ts › decodes the Gateway/Tomb/Deep Pool/plain NE |
| SC-3-3 | Area-card index 41 MUST be value 42 (EWU = E+W+stairUp), NOT 74 (EWD), because EWD has no tile in the set and would force an illegal rotation. | areaCards.ts:26 | gap-data-rng.test.ts |
| SC-3-4 | Special-type constants MUST be SPECIAL_NONE=0, GATEWAY=1, DEEP_POOL=2, VIPER_PIT=3, TOMB=4, GREAT_HALL=5. | areaCards.ts:1-6 | decode.test.ts (via SPECIAL_* imports) |
| SC-3-5 | The engine MUST define exactly 14 creatures (ids 0–13) with the normative fields fs, mp, carry, cost, points, flags, hostileMax, indiffMax, leaderPri (e.g. Hero fs5/cost6/pts10, Wizard mp5/cost null, Dragon fs6, Giant fs7/carry150, Sorcerer mp9). | creatures.ts:22-37 | data.test.ts › has 14 creatures with normative key stats |
| SC-3-6 | Creature flags MUST be the bitmask HUMAN=1, CHARISMA=2, BEFRIENDS_UNICORN=4, GUIDES_PAST_TRAP=8, INHUMAN=16. | creatures.ts:1-5 | data.test.ts (FLAG_CHARISMA, FLAG_GUIDES_PAST_TRAP) |
| SC-3-7 | A creature MUST be selectable as a starter iff `cost !== null`; only ids 0–7 have a non-null cost (Hero6, W-Hero5, Ogre5, Troll4, Priest4, Man3, Woman2, Dwarf1); ids 8–13 have cost null. | creatures.ts:22-37, setup.ts:24 | setup.test.ts › rejects a non-selectable creature (Wizard id 8) |
| SC-3-8 | The Dragon's reaction table MUST be hostileMax=4, indiffMax=6 (rolls 1–4 hostile, 5–6 indifferent, never friendly). | creatures.ts:33 | gap-data-rng.test.ts |
| SC-3-9 | Starting stock MUST be Hero×1, W-Hero×1, Ogre×3, Troll×3, Priest×3, Man×6, Woman×3, Dwarf×3 (8 entries, total 23). | creatures.ts:40-42 | data.test.ts › offers 8 selectable starters with the right stock |
| SC-3-10 | The engine MUST define exactly 15 treasures (ids 0–14); heavy = Silver/Gold/Gems (25 kg each) and Treasure Chest (100 kg); the other 12 are weightless artifacts. | treasures.ts:11-27 | data.test.ts › has 15 treasures and 5 hazards |
| SC-3-11 | Treasure Chest (id 14) MUST be kind "heavy", weight 100, points 0; Eye of God (id 13) MUST be points 0. | treasures.ts:25-26 | data.test.ts › TREASURES[14] Treasure Chest weight 100 heavy |
| SC-3-12 | The engine MUST define exactly 5 hazards ids 0–4: Mutiny, Trap, Earthquake, Medusa, Ghouls. | data/hazards.ts:2-7 | data.test.ts › has 15 treasures and 5 hazards |
| SC-3-13 | The small pack MUST be a single finite deck of exactly 71 cards: 37 creature cards (100+id), 27 treasure cards (200+id), 7 hazard cards (300+id). | smallPack.ts:8-40 | decks.test.ts › smallPackTemplate has 71 cards |
| SC-3-14 | Small-pack creature composition MUST be Hero×1, W-Hero×1, Ogre×3, Troll×3, Priest×3, Man×6, Woman×3, Dwarf×3, Wizard×3, Spectre×3, Dragon×3, Sorcerer×1, Giant×3, Unicorn×1 (37 total). | smallPack.ts:14-27 | decks.test.ts (exactly one W-Hero 101) |
| SC-3-15 | Small-pack treasure composition MUST be Silver×6, Gold×6, Gems×3, and 1× each of the 12 artifacts ids 3–14 (27 total). | smallPack.ts:29-32 | decks.test.ts › 27 treasure cards |
| SC-3-16 | Small-pack hazard composition MUST be Mutiny×1, Trap×2, Earthquake×2, Medusa×1, Ghouls×1 (7 total). | smallPack.ts:34-38 | decks.test.ts › 7 hazard cards |
| SC-3-17 | Card codes MUST be encoded as 100+creatureId (100–199), 200+treasureId (200–299), 300+hazardId (300–399), 400+creatureId (sleeping). | smallPack.ts:13-38, chamber.ts:6-8 | decks.test.ts (100/200/300 ranges) |
| SC-3-18 | A placed area MUST store `{card, coord, faceUp, visited, contents[], flags, indiffCount}` with optional dropped/markers/mirroredStairs/secretDoor fields. | state.ts:60-81 | setup.test.ts › places the Gateway and seats the chosen party |
| SC-3-19 | Coordinates MUST pack as `level*10000 + y*100 + x`; the Gateway MUST start at level 1, x=50, y=50 (packed 15050). | coords.ts:8-10, state.ts:9 | setup.test.ts › areas[0].coord == GATEWAY_START_COORD |
| SC-3-20 | Movement MUST map N→y−1, S→y+1, E→x+1, W→x−1, Up→level−1, Down→level+1 (DIR_N=1,E=2,S=3,W=4,UP=5,DOWN=6). | coords.ts:1-29 | gap-data-rng.test.ts |
| SC-3-21 | Party member status MUST be 0=original, 1=ally, 2=stone(petrified), 3=dead. There is NO party-size cap — the original rules impose no maximum (a friendly group is always added in full); the only natural bound is the finite small pack. A member MAY additionally record `borne?: number[]` — the subset of its carried treasure ids it is BEARING (wielded/worn/displayed); absent = all carried. Passive combat effects stay possession-based — `borne` governs only the item's fate when the holder falls (SC-7.3-13, SC-9.5-11). Multiplayer only: `mpTag?: string` (`"loan:<seat>"` \| `"recruit:<unionId>"`) is the union layer's identity tag (SC-MP-32); solo play never reads or writes it. (The other multi-only per-seat fields — `diceSeed`, `knownDoors`, `fleeGrace`, `forfeitTurnsOwed`, `sorcererSharedWith`, `seenAreas` — live on `PartyState` in multi.ts, documented at SC-MP-4 and SC-MP-28..37.) | state.ts:30-31,33-58 | gap-data-rng.test.ts; loot.test.ts › bears and stows a borneable item (Sword/Staff/Ring); multi-union.test.ts › mutiny regression |
| SC-3-22 | Game-state constants MUST be GS_PLAYING=0, GS_ESCAPED=1, GS_DEAD=2, GS_QUIT=3; AF_DESTROYED MUST be 4. | state.ts:1-4, state.ts:12 | gap-data-rng.test.ts |
| SC-3-23 | `newGame` MUST initialise turn=1, level=1, score=0, curses=0, bonusScore=0, partyArea=0, gs=GS_PLAYING, phase="explore", with the Gateway as the sole placed area (faceUp true, visited false). | setup.ts:58-88 | setup.test.ts › places the Gateway and seats the chosen party |
| SC-3-24 | Each new party member MUST start with status 0, dragonKills 0, empty treasure[]. | setup.ts:68-73 | setup.test.ts › party.every(status===0) |

## §4 The Turn Lifecycle & Action/Event Contract

| ID | Requirement | Code | Test |
|----|-------------|------|------|
| SC-4-1 | `reduce(state, action)` is the single pure entry point: it returns `{ state, events }`. The reducer is the only producer of game facts; the UI never infers them. | reduce.ts:540, actions.ts:44 | gap-contract.test.ts |
| SC-4-2 | If the game is over (`state.gs !== GS_PLAYING`), `reduce` ignores every action, returning the same state and an empty event list. | reduce.ts:555 | reduce.test.ts › ignores actions once the game is over |
| SC-4-3 | The interactive mode is `state.phase`, one of `explore | medusa | encounter | fight | pickup | gameOver`; it selects which controls show and which actions `reduce` accepts. `medusa` is the pre-hazard pause (SC-7.2-14). | state.ts:28 | gap-misc.test.ts; lotus-medusa.test.ts |
| SC-4-4 | `legalActions(state)` returns exactly the actions the UI may offer in the current phase; `reduce` validates against the same rules, rejecting any omitted action with `blocked` (or `planRejected`). This is the interactive contract. | selectors.ts:101-105 | reduce.test.ts › trap fall offers no withdraw; selectors.test.ts › interactive loop |
| SC-4-5 | `legalActions` returns `[]` when the game is over; quit is never an in-menu action (HUD button). | selectors.ts:106,132,152,214 | selectors.test.ts › no actions once over; reduce.test.ts › not.toContainEqual quit |
| SC-4-6 | An unrecognised action for the current phase produces one `{type:"blocked"}` event and no state change. | reduce.ts (phase guards, e.g. :534,613,641,749,796,880,924) | reduce.test.ts › resolveRound blocked when not fighting |
| SC-4-7 | `quit` (any phase): sets `gs=GS_QUIT`, `phase="gameOver"`, emits `gameOver(GS_QUIT)`. | reduce.ts:558 | reduce.test.ts › quit ends the game and emits gameOver(QUIT) |
| SC-4-8 | `exitCave`: legal only in explore; on level 1 with a printed stair-up sets `gs=GS_ESCAPED`, `phase="gameOver"`, emits `gameOver(GS_ESCAPED)`; else `blocked`. | reduce.ts:561 | reduce.test.ts › exitCave escapes when on level 1…; › blocked when no stair-up |
| SC-4-9 | `move(dir)`: legal in explore, OR in encounter while the one-shot `indiffLeaveOpen` window is open (leaving by any doorway, SC-4-18a). A successful move increments `turn` by 1, clears `fellThroughTrap`, then runs area resolution; a failed move does NOT advance the turn and emits `deadEnd` or `blocked`. | reduce.ts:583-590 | reduce.test.ts › a successful move increments the turn…; › dead-end does not advance the turn |
| SC-4-10 | Moving out (not reversing) of a Viper Pit emits `crossedSpecial(VIPER_PIT)` then runs the viper crossing (may wipe the party); out of a Deep Pool emits `crossedSpecial(DEEP_POOL)` then the deep-pool crossing. | reduce.ts:601,610 | reduce.test.ts › crossing a Deep Pool without a Giant drops heavy treasure; › going back the way you came does NOT trigger |
| SC-4-11 | `resolveArea` emits `moved(area,level)` first, then reviveStoned; resolves special markers, then (for a chamber) draws contents and applies hazards, then sets the resting phase. | reduce.ts:356-365 | reduce.test.ts › emits moved + drewChamber |
| SC-4-12 | On chamber entry `enterChamber` emits `drewChamber(strangers,treasures,hazards)`; then Eye-of-God annihilation, Talisman warding, and hazards fire in order. | reduce.ts:430-434, chamber.ts | reduce.test.ts › emits drewChamber |
| SC-4-13 | If a hazard leaves no living/ally member: `gs=GS_DEAD`, `phase="gameOver"`, emit `gameOver(DEAD)`; if every member is stone also emit `petrifiedOut`. | reduce.ts:277-283 | reduce.test.ts › Medusa turning the whole party to stone ends the game |
| SC-4-14 | A trap fall parks the chamber behind, relocates the party one level down (one-way, `fellThroughTrap=true`), emits `trapSprung(level)`+`moved`, and re-enters resolution at the lower level in the same turn (may chain). | reduce.ts:284-291,442 | reduce.test.ts › trap fall offers no withdraw; › falling leaves strangers/treasure behind |
| SC-4-15 | After resolution: strangers present → `encounter` (or `fight` if the area is in `hostileAreas`, surprise −1); else treasure → `pickup`; else → `explore`. A pacified area is parked and returns to `explore`. | reduce.ts:310-334 | reduce.test.ts › stranger→encounter; › treasure-only→pickup; › retreated re-entry→immediate fight |
| SC-4-16 | `surpriseReady` is set only on a fresh chamber entry (unused doorway / carpet) that is NOT a trap fall; testing reaction or starting the fight clears it. Extension kit fix: an extra draw into an ALREADY-entered chamber (Well/Bell, SC-EXT-7/SC-EXT-8) is never itself a fresh entry and must earn no surprise of its own, but it must also never CLOBBER surprise already earned by this chamber's original fresh entry and not yet consumed — `finishExtraDraw` captures `surpriseReady` as `hadSurprise` before calling `finishChamber(freshEntry=false, …, announceLull=true)` (which would otherwise force `surpriseReady` to `false` unconditionally AND suppress the `dragonsLulled` notice for a freshly-drawn Dragon) and restores `hadSurprise` afterward, but only while still in `encounter` phase (a Trap drawn via the extra draw resolves into a genuinely fresh landing area, which computes its OWN `surpriseReady` correctly via the normal `resolveAreaLoop` path and must not be overwritten). This same contract covers BOTH ways an extra draw can finish: directly (`resolveExtraDraw` calls `finishExtraDraw` immediately when no Medusa pause opens) and via a Medusa pause the draw itself opened (a drawn Medusa with Lotus Dust held pauses BEFORE `finishExtraDraw` runs; the pause's `medusaPause.extraDraw.hadSurprise` field carries the captured value through to `resumeFromMedusaPause`, which routes to the identical `finishExtraDraw` call on `proceed` or the Lotus throw) — a plain `{ freshEntry: false }` pause shape (as used by a genuine fresh-entry pause) would lose that context and reproduce the same clobber/suppression on resume, which is exactly the gap a first fix pass missed. | reduce.ts:327,148,844, reduce.ts:457-463,470-486, state.ts:161 | reduce.test.ts › attack from a fresh entry starts a fight with surprise; › attack with no fresh-entry surprise; kit-well-bell.test.ts › preserves an existing, unconsumed surpriseReady across a Well draw; › leaves surpriseReady false when it was false; › also preserves surpriseReady across a Bell Rope 'stir' draw; › announces dragonsLulled for a dragon drawn via the Well while the Charmed Flute is held; › preserves surpriseReady through the pause when resumed via proceed; › preserves surpriseReady through the same pause when resumed via the Lotus Dust throw; › announces dragonsLulled for a dragon drawn alongside a paused Medusa (Bell Rope 'stir' band) |
| SC-4-17 | `withdraw`: legal only in encounter; blocked if `fellThroughTrap`, `noWithdrawTurn` fired this turn (SC-EXT-9), or `prev` is earthquake-collapsed (`AF_DESTROYED`). On success parks the working set on the current tile, steps to `prev`, then re-resolves `prev` through the SAME shared landing resolution every other arrival uses (`resolveArea`, SC-4-11) — bug fix 2026-08-04 (Gap A): `withdraw` used to be the one landing path that skipped this and hardcoded `phase="explore"`, so an abandoned, not-yet-permanent encounter left behind at `prev` (SC-4-18a) silently vanished instead of re-opening; a `hostileAreas` tile never triggered its on-sight fight; and a Spell-remapped tile never revealed itself. All three now resolve correctly, uniformly, since every tile type funnels through the same code. | reduce.ts:729-758 | reduce.test.ts › withdraw steps back…; › blocked after trap; › blocked when earthquake collapsed the way back; › Gap A: withdrawing into an abandoned, not-yet-permanent encounter re-opens it, not explore; › Gap A: withdrawing into a hostileAreas tile (retreated from before) triggers an on-sight fight; › Gap A: withdrawing into a Spell-remapped tile reveals it (clears AF_UNRESOLVED) |
| SC-4-18 | `test`: legal only in encounter and while `indiffStreak < 3`; forfeits surprise; rolls a reaction and emits `reaction(outcome,roll)`. | reduce.ts:840 | reduce.test.ts › testing an always-hostile leader…; › three indifferent results pacify |
| SC-4-18a | Rules §Encountering Strangers — "in its next turn the party may test them again, or attack them, or leave the chamber by any doorway without picking up any treasure found there" — grants this once the FIRST test lands an indifferent result, not only the third/permanently-pacifying one (SC-4-20 is a separate, solitaire-only convenience rule about not needing to re-test on a later revisit; the old code wrongly conflated the two and required `indiffStreak >= 3` before any move). Bug fix 2026-08-04, round 2 (Gap C): the grant is a ONE-SHOT window (`state.indiffLeaveOpen`), not a standing condition of `indiffStreak >= 1` — an initial fix wrongly kept the leave option offered indefinitely, letting the party try doorway after doorway without ever re-testing. `indiffLeaveOpen` is set true only by a `test` resolving indifferent with the count still under 3, and is cleared (`delete`, never a bare `= false`, to avoid perturbing golden-fixture state hashes for every tested state — SC-EXT-1) by: a fresh `test` (recomputed from its own outcome), a fresh chamber entry (SC-8.4-5), or a dead-end leave attempt — "finds itself delayed by a dead end … must in the same turn either test the strangers again or attack them." While open, `legalActions`/`move` additionally allow leaving by any doorway/stair (excluding the level-1 stair-up cave exit, a separately-confirmed action), forfeiting the chamber's treasure; before any test (`indiffStreak` 0, window never opened) a fresh, untested encounter still forces withdraw/attack/test. Leaving parks strangers/treasures/hazards/sleeping/statues/lulled onto the vacated tile and switches to explore BEFORE attempting the move, mirroring an ordinary explore-phase move (a tunnel destination never runs `enterChamber`'s own reset, so the working set must be cleared first). A dead end (no move, tile unchanged, but a face-down frontier tile IS placed and the exit pruned, per ordinary exploration) reverts the park, closes the window, and returns the party to the SAME live encounter with test/attack/withdraw only. | selectors.ts:13-36,146-161; reduce.ts:583-635, 950-1030; chamber.ts:128-134; state.ts | reduce.test.ts › after ONE indifferent result the party may already move on by any doorway, leaving the treasure; › before any test, the party may NOT move on — must withdraw, attack, or test first; › a dead end while trying to leave an indifferent encounter re-opens the SAME encounter, not explore; › Gap C: choosing to test again after an indifferent result also forfeits the leave window |
| SC-4-19 | A `friendly` test recruits every eligible stranger as an ally (status 1 — no party-size limit), emits `strangersJoined`; a womanless Unicorn stays as a guard (`unicornGuards`), pacifying the area. Cleared strangers → pickup if treasure, else explore. | reduce.ts:848-875 | reduce.test.ts › a friendly result recruits the strangers as allies; › recruits EVERY stranger regardless of party size |
| SC-4-20 | An `indifferent` test increments `indiffStreak` and writes it through to the durable `indiffCounts[area]` (SC-8.4-5, bug fix 2026-08-04); on the 3rd, records the area in `pacifiedAreas`, emits `pacified`, parks strangers+treasure as guarded, returns to explore (permanently indifferent to this party). Otherwise stays in encounter and opens the one-shot leave window (SC-4-18a). | reduce.ts:999-1030 | reduce.test.ts › three indifferent results pacify the chamber |
| SC-4-21 | A `hostile` test starts a fight with surprise −1 (strangers gain surprise), emitting `fightStarted(-1)`. | reduce.ts:889-891 | reduce.test.ts › testing an always-hostile leader starts a fight with surprise to the strangers |
| SC-4-22 | `attack`: in encounter, starts a fight with surprise +1 if `surpriseReady` else 0. In explore on a pacified area with parked guards, un-parks guards+treasure and starts a fight with surprise 0. Otherwise `blocked`. Emits `fightStarted`. | reduce.ts:895-912 | reduce.test.ts › attack from a fresh entry…; › pacified chamber re-entry offers Attack |
| SC-4-23 | `startFight` sets `fight={surprise,round:1,focus}`, `phase="fight"`, clears `surpriseReady`/`fightDrops`, emits `fightStarted`. | reduce.ts:145-151 | reduce.test.ts (via arena) |
| SC-4-24 | `resolveRound(matches)`: legal only in fight, blocked if a casualty is pending. An illegal plan emits `planRejected(reason)` with no state change. A valid plan resolves one round, re-opens retreat, then pauses for `chooseCasualty` or finalises. | reduce.ts:914-925, combatPlan.ts | reduce.test.ts › illegal plan rejected; › legal plan clears the chamber; › blocked when not fighting |
| SC-4-25 | `finalizeRound`: a Unicorn may depart; no survivor → the fallen's carried items park on the tile (`sweepFallen`, SC-9.5-11) then `gameOver(DEAD)`; strangers cleared → `fightWon`, reclaim floor-dropped treasure and sweep the fallen's carried items into the pickup (SC-9.5-11), then pickup/explore; else the fight continues. | reduce.ts:185-217 | reduce.test.ts › wipes strangers wins; › wipes party ends DEAD; › winning reclaims floor-dropped treasure |
| SC-4-26 | `chooseCasualty(idx)`: legal only in fight with a pending `casualtyQueue`; `idx` must be a losing-pair member. Rolls d6 — 4-6 honours the pick, else the other falls; emits `casualtyChosen`, `memberDied`, Eye-forsaken. Empty queue → finalise. | reduce.ts:927-949 | reduce.test.ts › chooseCasualty falls on the player's pick with a 4-6 |
| SC-4-27 | `retreat(dir)`: legal only in fight; blocked if `fellThroughTrap` or before ≥1 round (`round<=1`). Flee by any exit. A failed retreat emits `deadEnd`, sets `retreatBlocked=true`. | reduce.ts:951-946 | reduce.test.ts › blocks retreat before any round; › dead-end retreat fails; › no tile to draw |
| SC-4-28 | A successful `retreat` leaves strangers and dropped/dead-carried treasure behind, clears the working set and `fight`, marks the fled area `hostileAreas`, and resolves the area retreated into. | reduce.ts:953-998 | reduce.test.ts › retreat flees by a doorway; › leaves a slain member's treasure behind |
| SC-4-29 | `takeTreasure(ti,mi)`: legal only in pickup. Lost Ruby (id 11) triggers a strength-8 statue fight (unless an Eye stills it): win → `rubyTaken`; loss → wrestler slain (`memberDied`+`statueAroused`), Ruby left, his carried items spilled into the pickup (SC-9.5-11). Other treasure assigned. Last treasure → explore. | reduce.ts:668-741 | reduce.test.ts › taking the last treasure returns to explore |
| SC-4-30 | `leaveTreasure`: legal only in pickup; parks remaining treasure and returns to explore. | reduce.ts:743-748 | reduce.test.ts › leaving treasure parks it on the chamber |
| SC-4-31 | `retakeDropped`: legal only in pickup; returns each fighter's dropped heavy treasure in its prior distribution, skipping any whose dropper fell / can't carry; emits `droppedRetaken`; if none, `blocked`. | reduce.ts:750-773 | reduce.test.ts › retakeDropped returns each fighter's dropped treasure |
| SC-4-32 | `moveTreasure(from,to,idx)`: blocked in fight/gameOver, when from==to, recipient non-living, or recipient can't carry — and blocked off a stone source or a corpse's borne item (SC-7.3-14); moving a borne item off a living member un-bears it, the recipient receives it carried (SC-7.3-13). Moving the Eye of God (13) curses the party (`curses+=1`, `eyeForsaken`). | reduce.ts:775-796 | reduce.test.ts › moves a treasure…; › blocks over-capacity; › blocks during a fight; › forsaking the Eye |
| SC-4-33 | `dropTreasure(mi,idx)`: blocked in fight/gameOver — and off a stone member or a corpse's borne item (SC-7.3-14); dropping a borne item un-bears it (SC-7.3-13). In pickup the item lands back on the live floor (re-takeable this visit); at rest (`explore`) with no active/guarded stranger markers on the tile it opens pickup immediately (SC-7.3-15, bug fix 2026-08-05); otherwise (a special sub-location, or a guarded/encounter tile) it parks on the tile. Dropping the Eye curses the party. | reduce.ts:892-931 | reduce.test.ts › bug fix 2026-08-05: dropping while at rest offers it straight back; › a drop onto a pacified area's still-guarded tile stays parked; › re-offers pickup-dropped; › forsaking the Eye |

Solo `reduce` never emits it. `medusaLooms` opens the medusa pause, `medusaSlept{until}` reports the Lotus throw, `medusaAsleep` an entry under her Lotus sleep (SC-7.2-14/15). `chasmDescend`/`whirlpoolRoll{roll,dragged}` are the kit's Chasm/Whirlpool events (SC-EXT-5/SC-EXT-6); `wellDraw`/`bellRoll{roll,outcome,creatureId}` are the kit's Well/Bell Rope events (SC-EXT-7/SC-EXT-8) — `bellRoll.creatureId` is the puller's creature id, not a party index (the index may go stale once the "vanish" outcome splices them out of `party`). `galleryStone{creatureIds}`/`staffWake{creatureIds}`/`lairStash{treasureIds}` are the kit's Gallery/Lair events (SC-EXT-10/SC-EXT-11/SC-EXT-12). `cryptParked` announces a freshly-parked Crypt/Gems card (never a reload); `cryptRoll{roll,outcome}` is the Crypt's trap/find band (both SC-EXT-13); `desertionRoll{creatureId,roll,deserted,items}` is Desertion's per-ally roll (with its carried-items snapshot, SC-EXT-14) — `desertionRoll.creatureId` is likewise a creature id, not a party index, for the same post-splice-safety reason as `bellRoll`. `wolfUnmoved{hazard}` is a Wolf's immunity skip, emitted by Medusa, Mutiny, AND Desertion alike (SC-EXT-14/SC-EXT-18) — `hazard` (a HAZARD_* id) names which one, so the presentation layer's Desertion-only "party holds together" summary can't mistake a Medusa/Mutiny skip for Desertion activity (review fix, Task 10). `harpiesSteal{treasureIds,cursed}`/`harpiesLurk` are Harpies' strike/park outcomes (SC-EXT-15) — `cursed` flags an Eye-of-God theft's own forsaken-curse wording, deliberately distinct from `eyeForsaken`. `quarrel{aId,bId,aRoll,bRoll,loserId}`/`quarrelFizzled` are Quarrel's duel and its too-few-combatants no-op (SC-EXT-16) — ids are creature ids, `loserId` null on a tie. `spellRemap{fizzled}` is the Spell hazard's remap-on-draw outcome, true/false covering the fizzle and the successful splice-and-replace alike (SC-EXT-28). `thiefPalmed{tid}` is a `takeTreasure` lift during a Thief-unlocked pickup session (`state.thiefPickup`, SC-EXT-19) — never fired for an ordinary pickup. `apprenticeTurned{count,items}`/`apprenticeStaysBehind{count}` are the Apprentice's Sorcerer-death revert and exit-cave desertion (both SC-EXT-20); `count` is normally 1 (a full-group friendly recruit could in principle bring more than one Apprentice into the same party). `demonSpawned`/`demonDispersed` are the Demon's draw-classify outcomes (a successful materialization into `prev`, or an earthquake-collapsed `prev` dispersing it instead); `demonUnfolds` is the forced-ambush notice fired whenever the party (re-)enters or withdraws into its area; `demonSlew{creatureId}` is the Demon's own Spectre-style auto-slay of the strongest member when it goes unfightable and unengaged — a separate event from `spectreSlew` only because its flavour text is Demon-specific (all four SC-EXT-21). `elixirDrunk{creatureId,roll,outcome}` is the Elixir's own outcome-band report (SC-EXT-22) — `outcome` mirrors the `roll`'s band (death/nothing/strength) as a discriminant; on the death band it fires ALONGSIDE the ordinary killing-die-roll machinery's own events (`deathPrevented`, or `eyeForsaken`/`itemsSpilled`), never in place of them. `holyWaterRevived{creatureId}`/`holyWaterStatueWoke{creatureId}`/`holyWaterMedusaDestroyed`/`holyWaterFoeDestroyed{creatureId}`/`holyWaterWeakened{creatureId}` are Holy Water's five per-mode outcomes (SC-EXT-24) — `holyWaterMedusaDestroyed` alone carries no `creatureId` (Medusa is a hazard, not a creature). `scrollRead{destroyed,survivors}` is the Scroll's outcome (SC-EXT-25) — both arrays are creature ids (duplicates allowed); the party is ALWAYS cursed on a legal use (`state.curses+=1`), reported by the presentation layer's own standing-curse notice rather than a boolean field on this event. `shieldWarded{creatureId,mode}` is the Magic Shield's pairing-scoped ward notice (SC-EXT-27) — `mode` "nullify" (an ordinary foe's mp fully zeroed) or "weaken" (the Sorcerer/Apprentice's own −2, stacking with Lotus Dust/Holy Water/Eye, floor 0) — fired once per stranger the ward actually reduced (base mp>0) in a resolved round, never merely because the Shield is in play. `islandJump{special}` narrates a `jumpToIsland` (SC-10.5-5) — the mechanical roll/drop is reported by the SAME `viperPit`/`treasureDropped` events an ordinary crossing already emits (`special.ts` reused verbatim), so this event alone distinguishes "jumped to the island" from "crossed and left." `pendingDropDelivered{treasureIds}` fires when a Chasm/Whirlpool drop queued in `state.pendingDrops` lands at its target coordinate on a genuine (re-)entry (SC-10.5-16, special-areas revision 2026-08-08) — distinct from `treasureReclaimed`/`droppedRetaken`, which are Deep-Pool/Viper-Pit-only reclaim paths with an eligible-carrier gate this delivery has none of. | actions.ts:46-261 | gap-contract.test.ts; | SC-4-43 | `reduce`'s result carries an optional `midState`: a `structuredClone` of the state captured at `relocateDown`'s entry, present exactly when resolving the action relocated the party AFTER the current area's events had fired (a Trap during a chamber draw — including via a Well/Bell extra draw — a Whirlpool drag, a Chasm descent, a Crypt fall; all relocations funnel through `relocateDown`). It is presentation-only: never part of `GameState`, never persisted, ignored by `replay`/conformance (frames pin `state`+`events` only), and absent whenever no relocation interrupted the action. The UI holds it as the backdrop so dice/notices present against the room they happened in, not the landing tile (docs/bugs/ZTNU-log.json: a Trap's same-draw Mutiny appeared to fire "in a tunnel" one level below). | reduce.ts:489-513,540-552 | midState.test.ts › a trap drawn on chamber entry returns midState at the entered chamber, final state at the landing; › a plain move (no relocation) returns no midState |

## §5 Randomness & Shuffling

| ID | Requirement | Code | Test |
|----|-------------|------|------|
| SC-5-1 | Party selection MUST reject an empty party and any pick whose creature id is not a selectable starter (cost null/undefined, resolved via `selectionCost` since SC-EXT-29). | setup.ts:19-24 | setup.test.ts › rejects empty party; › rejects Wizard id 8 |
| SC-5-2 | Party selection MUST reject a total cost exceeding PARTY_BUDGET=6. | setup.ts:7,28 | setup.test.ts › rejects two Priests = 8 |
| SC-5-3 | Party selection MUST reject picking more copies of a creature than STARTING_STOCK (or, kit-on, `startingStock(variants)`, SC-EXT-29) allows. | setup.ts:29-32 | setup.test.ts › rejects two Heroes |
| SC-5-4 | `newGame` MUST throw on invalid picks before building any decks. | setup.ts:44 | setup.test.ts › throws on invalid picks |
| SC-5-5 | The party MUST be drawn FROM the shuffled small pack: each chosen creature's card (100+id) MUST be removed once, so a picked card can never appear as a chamber stranger. | setup.ts:52-56 | setup.test.ts › removes the chosen party cards from the small pack |
| SC-5-6 | The LCG MUST advance as seed ← (seed × 1103515245 + 12345) mod 2^31 (glibc constants), computed in BigInt to avoid 32-bit overflow. | rng.ts:4-11 | rng.test.ts › nextSeed matches the glibc LCG recurrence (nextSeed(1)=1103527590) |
| SC-5-7 | `rollDie` MUST advance the seed, extract upper bits 15..30 (`floor(s/32768) % 65536`), and return `min(5, floor(bits/10923)) + 1`, uniform on 1..6. | rng.ts:14-19 | rng.test.ts › rollDie deterministic; covers full 1..6 range |
| SC-5-8 | `randBelow(seed,n)` MUST return {seed,0} unchanged when n≤0; else advance the seed and return `bits % n`. | rng.ts:22-27 | rng.test.ts › returns [0,n); returns 0 for n≤0 without advancing |
| SC-5-9 | `shuffle` MUST be Fisher–Yates iterating i from length−1 down to 1, drawing j=randBelow(i+1), swapping arr[i]↔arr[j]; pure (new array, input unmutated) and threads the advanced seed. | rng.ts:30-42 | rng.test.ts › is a permutation; does not mutate input; deterministic |
| SC-5-10 | `buildLargePack` MUST shuffle the 60 area-card values (Gateway removed) and return the advanced seed; result is a permutation with no 175. | decks.ts:11-16 | decks.test.ts › 60 cards, no Gateway; deterministic |
| SC-5-11 | `buildSmallPack` MUST shuffle all 71 template cards and return the advanced seed, preserving the multiset. | decks.ts:19-25 | decks.test.ts › preserves the template multiset |
| SC-5-12 | `newGame` MUST consume RNG in order: large pack from the input seed → small pack from the large pack's advanced seed → store the small pack's advanced seed as `state.seed`; largeIdx/smallIdx start at 0. | setup.ts:46-47,89-96 | setup.test.ts › builds a 60-card large pack and small pack minus party |
| SC-5-13 | The engine MUST be fully deterministic from `seed`: no Math.random/Date.now; RNG state lives only in `state.seed` (the seed is a caller-supplied parameter, not clock-derived). | rng.ts:1-3 | rng.test.ts (deterministic cases) |
| SC-5-14 | `replay(seed, picks, actions, variants?)` MUST reconstruct a game move-by-move by folding `reduce` over the action log, returning `actions.length + 1` frames: frame 0 = `newGame(seed, picks, variants)` (action null, no events); frame i = state after `actions[0..i-1]` with the i-th action + its events. Being a pure fold, it reproduces the exact states and consequences of the original play — the basis of the game log's from-scratch, forward/backward replay. `variants` (SC-EXT-29) is threaded straight into the internal `newGame` call; omitted, it is identical to calling `replay` with no 4th argument at all. | replay.ts:31-45 | replay.test.ts › reproduces the exact final state and per-move events from the action log alone; › frame 0 is the untouched initial state; › deterministic; › seed sweep; › threads variants into newGame so a kit-on game's decks/state reconstruct exactly; › omitting variants (old codes) decodes kit-off — identical to no fourth argument at all |

| SC-7.3-12 | Re-entering a passage tile (tunnel / Gateway — `!chamber`, and not a Pool/Pit) that has floor treasure parked on `area.contents` (200+tid) reloads it into `state.treasures`, removes those codes, and enters `pickup` so it can be reclaimed. Without this, treasure LEFT (via `leaveTreasure`, or dropped onto a guarded tile) on a passage tile would be stranded, because a passage tile never runs `enterChamber` (which is what reloads a chamber's parked floor). Mirrors the Deep-Pool/Viper-Pit stationary-reclaim precedent (SC-10.5-13) generalized to ordinary chambers and passage tiles: the party never actually left the room, so there is no reason to force a leave-and-return round trip through `resolveArea`/`enterChamber` just to notice treasure that never left its sight. A guarded tile (a `pacifiedAreas` chamber whose guards are still parked, 100-199 codes present) is deliberately excluded — a fresh drop there must not open a free bypass around the "must attack to loot guarded treasure" rule (SC-4-20); it parks exactly as before, same as a drop mid-encounter. | reduce.ts:904-926 | reduce.test.ts › bug fix 2026-08-05: dropping while at rest offers it straight back; › bug fix 2026-08-05: the same fix applies to a genuine CHAMBER (not just a tunnel/Gateway); › a drop onto a pacified area's still-guarded tile stays parked; precise-locations.test.ts › an ordinary (non-special) chamber is unaffected — a drop never sinks, and opens pickup at rest |

The Magic Sword (3), Magic Staff (9) and The Ring (10) have a borne mode; the extension kit's Magic Axe (17) and Magic Shield (20) join the SAME mode, mirrored exactly (`BORNEABLE = [3, 9, 10, 17, 20]`, design US-23/US-24, SC-EXT-26/27) — everything else is always carried. Blocked in fight/gameOver; the member must be living (status 0/1); bearing requires a borneable item not already borne; stowing requires it currently borne — with NO creature-type restriction of its own, so the Shield (whose ward is eligible-bearer-only, SC-EXT-27) is bearable by ANY member (design Resolved-15). Like `moveTreasure` it is a party-panel action, never offered via `legalActions`. Handing over (`moveTreasure`) or dropping (`dropTreasure`) a borne item un-bears it first — the recipient receives it merely carried. Passive combat effects (Sword/Axe bonus, Staff MP, Ring rolls, Shield ward) remain possession-based; `borne` governs ONLY the item's fate when its holder falls (SC-7.2-13, SC-9.5-11). | reduce.ts:820-838, loot.ts:20-22, reduce.ts:791,809 | loot.test.ts › bears and stows a borneable item (Sword/Staff/Ring); › rejects bearing a non-borneable item, mid-fight use, and dead/stone members; › handing over or dropping a borne item un-bears it; kit-axe-shield.test.ts › is BORNEABLE and petrifies WITH a stoned bearer (Axe); › is BORNEABLE, and setBorne is legal for ANY member — even one ineligible for the ward (Shield) |

| SC-8.4-5 | Bug fix 2026-08-04 (Gap D): the indifference count is DURABLE and persists across visits — "they remember forever how many times your party has approached them ... even if you went away in between" (Peter's clarification). `indiffCounts[area]` (per-party, keyed by area index) holds this durable value; `indiffStreak` is the live working copy, restored from it on every chamber entry (`state.indiffCounts?.[state.partyArea] ?? 0`), fresh or reload alike — NOT reset to 0 unconditionally, as an earlier pass of this same bug fix mistakenly believed already matched the rule. The one-shot leave window (`indiffLeaveOpen`, SC-4-18a) is separately always reset to false on entry regardless of the restored count — however high the durable count, re-entering still requires a fresh test/attack before any free leave. Only `pacifiedAreas` (the count reaching 3) additionally short-circuits future entries entirely (SC-4-20, SC-8.5-7). | chamber.ts:128-134 | reduce.test.ts › a second, separate approach resumes the count instead of restarting at 0; › three SEPARATE indifferent approaches (leaving and returning each time) reach permanent pacification; gap-misc.test.ts › enterChamber restores a pre-set indiffStreak to 0 when no durable count is recorded for this area; › enterChamber RESTORES the durable count when this area has one |

| SC-9.5-9 | Fight-end (`finalizeRound`): no living party → the fallen's carried items park on the tile (SC-9.5-11) then DEAD/gameOver; all strangers dead → `fightWon`, reclaim floor treasure + sweep the fallen's carried items into the pickup (SC-9.5-11), then pickup (if treasure) or explore; else the round advanced and fighting continues. | SC-9.5-11 | Unified death-loot rule (`sweepFallen`): every dead (status 3) member's CARRIED items spill off the body — on a WON fight to the live floor (`state.treasures`), joining the post-win pickup (this fixes the previously-silent loss of a slain member's artifacts on a won solo fight and gives the §489 Eye-on-a-corpse a recovery path); on a WIPE to the tile (`area.contents`, 200+tid) for a later party, before `gameOver`. One `itemsSpilled{creatureId,items}` per member with anything to spill; idempotent across repeat sweeps. BORNE items are lost with the body in both cases. Also fired on the lost Ruby wrestle (SC-7.3-9) and the Ghouls death loop (SC-7.2-6). Exceptions: viper-pit deaths lose everything INTO the pit — a fall into the pit is not a lootable floor (`m.treasure=[]`, SC-10.1-3) — and a retreat leaves behind ALL of the perished's artifacts, even borne (§426, SC-9.5-8). | loot.ts:40-51, reduce.ts:194,208 | loot.test.ts › on a WON fight the fallen's CARRIED items join the pickup; a borne item is lost with the body; › on a WIPE the fallen's carried items park on the tile for whoever comes next |

| SC-10.5-3 | The four special areas (Deep Pool, Viper Pit, Whirlpool, Chasm) are always `doorway` or `island` — never `centre`. | subLocation.ts:80-88 | precise-locations.test.ts › a special area is never 'centre' |
| SC-10.5-4 | `island` is derived from a vertical arrival (the party's `prev` is on a different level — a secret stair) or `fellThroughTrap` (a trap fall); `doorway` direction is derived by comparing the current area's coord to `prev`'s (which lateral neighbour it arrived from). | subLocation.ts:38-70 | precise-locations.test.ts › derives doorway direction…/island when the arrival was vertical/whenever fellThroughTrap is set |
| SC-10.5-5 | `jumpToIsland` (Peter's house rule, designer-approved 2026-07-30 — NOT the printed rulebook): jump from a doorway onto the island without leaving the tile. Legal only in `explore`, standing in a doorway of a Viper-Pit or Deep-Pool tile (not Whirlpool — no island; not Chasm — reached only via a secret stair/trap, never a jump); sets the explicit override `GameState.subLocation={area,at:"island"}` without touching `partyArea`/`prev`. | actions.ts (jumpToIsland), reduce.ts (case "jumpToIsland"), selectors.ts (ISLAND_JUMP_SPECIALS) | precise-locations.test.ts › jumpToIsland |
| SC-10.5-6 | The jump reuses the ordinary crossing's own risk verbatim: Viper Pit calls `viperCrossing` (same per-creature fatal 1-or-2 d6, SC-10.1-1, including a total-wipe `gameOver`); Deep Pool calls `deepPoolCrossing` (same non-Giant-carried heavy-treasure auto-drop, SC-10.2-2, no roll). Bug fix 2026-08-08 (special-areas revision): `jumpToIsland` now also costs a turn (`state.turn += 1`), matching every other way of changing position — previously it was the one form of movement that left `turn` untouched, a free action relative to an ordinary crossing. | reduce.ts (case "jumpToIsland") | precise-locations.test.ts › Viper Pit: reuses…/a fatal roll can wipe…; Deep Pool: auto-drops…/a Giant carries everything; › jumpToIsland costs a turn, like any other move |
| SC-10.5-7 | The override is invalidated by any REAL position change — cleared in the ordinary "move" case, `relocateDown`, and `carpetMove` — so a stale `island` override never survives a later revisit to the same area index. | reduce.ts (move case, relocateDown, carpetMove) | precise-locations.test.ts › a stale override left over from a different area is ignored |
| SC-10.5-8 | Viper Pit / Whirlpool ledge crossing is **adjacency-gated**: from a doorway, only the two ADJACENT compass directions (and retracing) are legal — the ONE direction directly opposite (`oppositeDir`) is illegal, enforced in both `legalActions` (hidden from the UI) and the reducer's "move" case (rejected even if dispatched directly). Deep Pool ("swim to any of the other doorways") and Chasm (no lateral crossing modelled at all, unchanged) have no such limit; a crossing sourced from the island is always unrestricted. | subLocation.ts (RING_ADJACENCY_SPECIALS, oppositeDir), selectors.ts (legalActions), reduce.ts (case "move") | precise-locations.test.ts › adjacency-gated crossing |
| SC-10.5-9 | Precise dropped treasure: a deliberate `dropTreasure` at rest/encounter on **Deep Pool or Viper Pit** sinks into `PlacedArea.sunkTreasure`, bucketed by the sub-location it was cast from (a doorway direction or the island) — distinct from Deep Pool's automatic, un-bucketed `dropped` pile (SC-10.2-2). **Chasm and Whirlpool no longer sink into `sunkTreasure` at all** (special-areas revision, bug fix 2026-08-08) — see SC-10.5-16. A drop during an active pickup is unaffected (still the live floor, as any ordinary drop). An ordinary (non-special) chamber/tunnel never sinks either way — but since bug fix 2026-08-05 (SC-7.3-15) it is NOT simply "still ordinary `contents`" at rest: unguarded, it opens pickup immediately instead of parking. | reduce.ts (case "dropTreasure") | precise-locations.test.ts › precise dropped treasure — sinking a deliberate drop; › an ordinary (non-special) chamber is unaffected — a drop never sinks, and opens pickup at rest |
| SC-10.5-10 | Reclaim is gated per area, checked on (re-)entry (and, since bug fix 2026-08-02, also while simply parked on the tile — SC-10.5-13): Deep Pool is **Giant-only** (mirrors SC-10.2-4, reusing `giantCanRecover`) and Viper Pit needs a **Charmed-Flute-eligible carrier** (reusing `fluteLulls` — the rulebook's own condition for pit treasure, extended here from fatal-fall losses to deliberate drops); both checked in `resolveAreaLoop` alongside the pre-existing `dropped` reclaim. Whirlpool/Chasm have no reclaim mechanic of their own at all any more — their drops never land at THIS tile in the first place (SC-10.5-16). | reduce.ts (resolveAreaLoop, SPECIAL_DEEP_POOL/SPECIAL_VIPER_PIT branches) | precise-locations.test.ts › precise dropped treasure — reclaim on (re)entry |
| SC-10.5-11 | Reclaim is scoped to the CURRENT sub-location's own bucket only — a different doorway's stash is untouched and waits for a future visit. (D) DECISION / named simplification: only one bucket reclaims per visit (the automatic `dropped` pile takes priority over a `sunkTreasure` bucket at the same sub-location) — a rare simultaneous double-stash waits for a later trip rather than being merged into one pickup. | reduce.ts (resolveAreaLoop) | precise-locations.test.ts › leaving other doorways' stashes untouched |
| SC-10.5-12 | Multiplayer inherits the whole model for free: `GameState.subLocation` reaches every seat's `PartyState` via `PartyCore` (`Omit<GameState, cave fields>`, multi.ts's `compose`/`splitCave`) with no dedicated wiring; `PlacedArea.sunkTreasure` is genuinely cave-shared (lives on `CaveState.areas`, like `contents`/`dropped`), so one seat's drop is visible and reclaimable by another. `pendingDrops` (SC-10.5-16, special-areas revision 2026-08-08) is a `GameState` scalar, not `PlacedArea` content, so it does NOT ride along for free the way `sunkTreasure` does — it needs the SAME dedicated `CaveState`/`compose`/resync wiring `cryptCoord` already established (SC-EXT-32): `CaveState.pendingDrops` is seeded fresh from the cave on every `compose()` call and resynced from the acting seat's result after every action, so a drop queued by one seat is delivered to whichever seat's own exploration reaches the target coordinate first. | multi.ts:76-79,186-206 (subLocation/sunkTreasure, free); multi.ts (`CaveState.pendingDrops`, `compose`, post-action resync — dedicated wiring, mirroring `cryptCoord`) | precise-locations-multi.test.ts; precise-locations-multi.test.ts › special-areas revision (2026-08-08): pending Chasm/Whirlpool drops are cave-shared |
| SC-10.5-13 | Bug fix 2026-08-02: reclaim is ALSO offered while the party is simply PARKED (`explore` phase) at a Deep Pool/Viper Pit sub-location holding reclaimable treasure — not just on (re-)entry (SC-10.5-10). Before this fix, a party that dropped treasure there themselves, or was resting on an earlier visit's still-sunk stash, had no legal way to pick it up without first leaving by a doorway and walking back in. A new `reclaimTreasure` action (no params) is legal in `explore` phase on one of these two specials whenever the auto-dropped pile or the sunk bucket at the party's EXACT current sub-location holds an eligible carrier's worth of treasure; `legalActions` mirrors the same eligibility check read-only. The reclaim logic itself (`tryReclaimSunk`) is factored out of `resolveAreaLoop`'s own Deep-Pool/Viper-Pit branches into `special.ts`, shared by both the (re-)entry path and this new stationary action, so the two can never diverge. | special.ts:19-70 (`giantCanRecover`, `sunkKey`, `tryReclaimSunk`), reduce.ts:677-691 (`reclaimTreasure` case), selectors.ts:233-243 (read-only mirror), actions.ts (`reclaimTreasure`) | precise-locations.test.ts › stationary reclaim — Deep Pool/Viper Pit; apps/web ExplorePanel.test.tsx › offers 'Reclaim the sunk treasure' … |
| SC-10.5-16 | Special-areas revision (bug fix 2026-08-08): the designer's own dissatisfaction with "you may not drop treasure into Chasm/Whirlpool" (docs/requirements/bug-fixes/2026-08-08-special-areas.md) is resolved by letting a deliberate `dropTreasure` FALL THROUGH to the level below, once that level has genuinely been explored — rather than sinking into `PlacedArea.sunkTreasure` like Deep Pool/Viper Pit (SC-10.5-9, from which Chasm/Whirlpool are now excluded) or reusing the Lair/Harpies' per-party holding-pen model (SC-EXT-12, rejected: unlike the Lair's genuinely unknowable-until-drawn coordinate, a Chasm/Whirlpool's level-below coordinate is always immediately computable — `level+1` at the same x,y — so there is no genuine "not yet in play" gap to hold a stash against). `dropTreasure`'s Chasm/Whirlpool branch computes the target coordinate directly: if an area already exists there AND has been `visited`, the treasure lands immediately as ordinary floor content (`200+tid`) on that area; otherwise it queues onto the new `GameState.pendingDrops` map (keyed by packed target coordinate, Appendix A) rather than being lost or landing on an unvisited/nonexistent tile. Delivery is a hook at the very top of `resolveAreaLoop`'s loop, running before any other branch (special, tunnel, or chamber): whenever the party's CURRENT tile has a pending queue, every treasure id in it is pushed onto `contents` and the queue entry is deleted, emitting `pendingDropDelivered{treasureIds}` — landing exactly like any other floor treasure for the existing tunnel/chamber content-reload pass that immediately follows (no special-casing needed: it sweeps into the live pickup working set the same way a fresh chamber's own contents would). This also means Whirlpool/Chasm have NO reclaim mechanic of their own any more (SC-10.5-10) — a drop simply waits at its destination for WHICHEVER party or seat gets there first, cave-shared in multiplayer (SC-10.5-12). | reduce.ts (case "dropTreasure", the Chasm/Whirlpool fall-through branch; `resolveAreaLoop`'s delivery hook at the top of its loop), state.ts (`pendingDrops`), actions.ts (`pendingDropDelivered` event) | precise-locations.test.ts › Whirlpool/Chasm: a deliberate drop never sinks here — it queues for the level below (not yet explored); › delivers IMMEDIATELY if the level below has already been explored; › a pending drop delivers the moment its target coordinate is genuinely entered, via ordinary exploration; › Chasm: descendChasm lands the party right on top of its OWN pending drop, which delivers immediately; precise-locations-multi.test.ts › special-areas revision (2026-08-08): pending Chasm/Whirlpool drops are cave-shared |

| SC-11-43 | Bug fix 2026-08-09: a vertical carpet teleport (`carpetMove`, DOWN or UP) onto UNEXPLORED space no longer OR-s a phantom return-stair bit onto the freshly drawn card — the card is placed exactly as drawn/printed, with `mirroredStairs` left absent. The rulebook is explicit: "you may not withdraw, and the carpet remains behind" (§Magic carpet) — inventing a stair the party could later retrace by ordinary movement was exactly the secret door the printed rules never grant. This is distinct from SC-6.1-13/14's own mirrored-stair mechanic, which remains correct and untouched: THAT mirroring exists so an ordinary stair move never stumbles into a stranding dead end, a real gap the carpet — which ignores doors on the way OUT and was never intended to guarantee a way back at all — never had. A lateral (N/E/S/W) carpet move was never affected either way (no stair bits are ever OR-ed in for those directions). | reduce.ts:536-558 (`carpetMove`) | carpet.test.ts › bug fix 2026-08-09: a vertical teleport onto unexplored space mirrors no return stair |
| SC-11-44 | Bug fix 2026-08-09 (QOTO-02/03): using the Magic Carpet used to `consume()` it exactly like a genuinely single-use artifact (Lotus Dust, Strength Potion, Healing Balm) — permanently deleting it from the game, observed as "the carpet disappeared." The card is explicit: "the carpet must be left in the area where it was used, but can be retrieved and used again." Fixed by pushing an ordinary `200+4` floor-treasure code onto the VACATED area's `contents` — computed from `next.partyArea` BEFORE `carpetMove` changes it — the instant it's removed from the bearer's carried treasure, using the exact same parked-floor-treasure representation `leaveTreasure`/`dropTreasure`'s ordinary-area branch already use (never the special-area sink/fall-through machinery — SC-10.5-9/16 — since the rulebook draws no such distinction for this). No new event or field: the existing `carpetUsed` notice already fires, and the area's own ordinary chamber/tunnel content-reload cycle (SC-7.1, SC-6-7 narrative) picks it back up as ordinary pickup treasure on any later (re-)visit — including a same-turn `withdraw` right back onto the vacated tile, which is now the observable proof this works (gap-contract.test.ts's own SC-4-40 case). | reduce.ts:1273-1291 (`case 4` in `useArtifact`) | carpet.test.ts › teleports to an existing adjacent area ignoring doors, leaving the carpet behind; › bug fix 2026-08-09 (QOTO-02/03): the carpet remains behind, not destroyed › left as ordinary floor treasure on the vacated tile — never lands on the destination; › can be retrieved on a later visit; gap-contract.test.ts › SC-4-40: the Magic-Carpet 'no withdraw after landing into strangers' deviation is NOT enforced |

## §12 Scoring & Game Over

| ID | Requirement | Code | Test |
|----|-------------|------|------|

| SC-MP-32 | Unions (spec I-6/I-7/I-8, M5) — the LOAN model: on formation each subordinate's LIVING members move into the commander's party array, tagged `PartyMember.mpTag = "loan:<seat>"` (recruits `"recruit:<id>"`); `mpTag` is IDENTITY, so `reindexUnion` re-derives every loan/recruit position from tags after EVERY commander action — solo rules (e.g. a Mutiny desertion) splice the array, so positions cannot be trusted (the mutiny regression). Returns and allocations select by tag. Joining charges a one-turn forfeit fee (`forfeitTurnsOwed`); a subordinate's own turn is skipped (never plays solo — the commander moves the combined force); the commander's PvP engage obeys the strongest-fights-strongest fairness check; recruited allies are recorded on the union and allocated by handshake after dissolution — a DISAGREEMENT parks the recruit as a neutral stranger on the tile; `divideParty` posts rear-guard detachments (deter-only: `hostileDetachmentAt` blocks rivals' pickup, no defence fight — TODO M6+); a commander going terminal auto-dissolves the union (loans return, dead members are the owner's loss). Because the loan model composes the union AS the commander's own party array, solo party-scoped rules fired by a commander's action operate on the COMBINED force with no fork — see SC-EXT-34 for the kit's Quarrel and the loan-ending removal rules. | multi-union.ts:89-547, multi-fight.ts:334-343, multi.ts:243-249,412-425,432-435,550-551, state.ts:57 | multi-union.test.ts › union formation; › union turn logic & combined force; › union PvP command (M5 fairness); › division & rear-guards; › loan-index stability under solo array reshaping (mutiny regression) |

| SC-MP-34 | Split RNG (M6, spec §0 principle 2): `buildMpGame` derives seat k's `PartyState.diceSeed` = `nextSeed`^(k+1)(cave.seed) — DERIVED, never consumed from the cave stream; PvP rolls consume the rolling side's COMMAND LEAD's `diceSeed` substream, so an inter-party round never perturbs the shared `cave.seed`; parties without a `diceSeed` (pre-M6 games) fall back to the shared cave stream (pinned). | multi.ts:100-107,274-288, multi-fight.ts:406-425 | multi-concurrent.test.ts › split RNG (M6, spec §0 principle 2) |
| SC-MP-35 | Concurrent mode (M6, per-game flag `concurrent`): flag off is byte-identical strict round-robin; flag on drops the turn gate — free-roam seats act any time, the shared deck serialises in action-arrival order, and `turnCount` ticks once per completed solo action. Sessions lock ALL their participants (no turn boundary to police a walk-away); a seat may not move INTO an area with a rival's live stranger-fight (I-13 extended); `fleeGrace` degrades to a pursuit lockout (`declareAttack` barred until the grace clears on the same clean/dirty rules); forfeits are paid down one per OTHER seat's completed action (an owing seat may not initiate, but is never dead-stopped when no exploring rival remains); `endTurn` is a harmless no-op; the all-terminal finish is detected directly (no `advanceTurn`). | multi.ts:133-140,428-429,436-457,470-471,482-501,596-611, multi-fight.ts:201-205 | multi-concurrent.test.ts › concurrent exploration (M6, per-game flag) |

Every seat's `seenAreas` ledger records the areas it has stood in (recorded ALWAYS, applied only under fogLite); `fogFilter(mp, seat)` masks unseen areas to face-down stubs, and `distantFights(mp, seat)` counts rival fights outside the seat's view. `extensionKit` widens the shared decks (SC-EXT-4) and is threaded into every composed seat's `GameState.variants` by `compose()` (SC-EXT-30) — it plays no part in fog/zombies, which stay keyed on `mp.variants` directly. | multi.ts:108-112,141-149,264-269,284-299,632-680 | multi-zombies.test.ts › fog-of-war-lite (M7, plan ⑦); multi-kit.test.ts › kit on: buildMpGame(seed, seats, { extensionKit: true }) stores the flag on MpGameState.variants |

## §EXT Extension kit

| ID | Requirement | Code | Test |
|----|-------------|------|------|
| SC-EXT-1 | Solo `GameState` gains `variants?: { extensionKit?: boolean }`, mirroring `MpGameState.variants` (multi.ts:133). `newGame(seed, picks, variants?)` stores it verbatim (omitted when the caller passes no third argument or an explicit falsy variants); it is immutable for the life of the game — no reducer path writes it. `buildLargePack`/`buildSmallPack` accept the same optional variants parameter (gates the kit's deck content, SC-EXT-4; `validatePicks`/`newGame` route selection through it too, SC-EXT-29). Absent, `{}`, or `{extensionKit:false}` MUST leave gameplay byte-identical to calling `newGame`/`buildLargePack`/`buildSmallPack` with no variants argument at all — the same deck arrays, same shuffles, same resulting state (aside from the `variants` bookkeeping field itself). | state.ts:182-185, setup.ts:36-38,98, decks.ts:5-25 | kit-variant.test.ts › newGame(seed, picks) with no variants arg is identical to passing variants explicitly absent; › newGame(seed, picks) produces the same state whether or not a third arg is passed at all — byte identity; › newGame(seed, picks, { extensionKit: true }) stores the flag, and it round-trips JSON; › newGame(seed, picks, {}) or ({extensionKit: false}) leaves gameplay identical (decks, party, gateway); › buildSmallPack(seed) ≡ buildSmallPack(seed, undefined) ≡ buildSmallPack(seed, {}) — identical packs; › buildLargePack(seed) ≡ buildLargePack(seed, undefined) ≡ buildLargePack(seed, {}) — identical packs |

A fresh draw of code `221` (`chamber.ts`'s `classify`) does NOT lie on the floor: it parks by setting `state.cryptCoord` to the drawing area's coord — a single coordinate (mirroring `lairCoord`, SC-EXT-12) rather than a chamber-working-set bucket or a re-parked content code, since there is exactly one such card in the kit's small pack (at most one crypt ever exists) and, unlike Medusa/Ghouls, it never re-fires automatically — it waits for the deliberate `enterCrypt` action. The park is announced (`cryptParked`, design US-08 on-screen text: "A sealed crypt squats in the corner of this chamber.") exactly once, for whichever draw actually sets `cryptCoord` — `enterChamber`'s first-visit draw and the Well/Bell extra-draw sites (`drawFromWell`/`pullBellRope`, reduce.ts) each snapshot `cryptCoord` immediately before their own draw and compare after, so an unrelated LATER Well/Bell draw elsewhere never re-announces an already-parked crypt (a bare post-draw truthiness check would have false-positived on every subsequent draw once the crypt existed anywhere on the map). A new action `enterCrypt` is legal only at rest (`explore` phase — "the start of any turn", design US-08; deliberately gated tighter than the Chasm/Well/Bell Rope's mid-encounter escape-hatch latitude, since the design gives the Crypt no "legal mid-encounter too" note) while standing on the area `cryptCoord` names, and clears `cryptCoord` immediately on firing, whatever the roll — no second entry. One visible d6 (`cryptRoll{roll,outcome}`): **1-2 — "trap"**: an UNAVOIDABLE whole-party `relocateDown` (a living Dwarf's `GUIDES_PAST_TRAP` does not apply — this path never touches `applyHazards`' HAZARD_TRAP case at all), landing with `fellThroughTrap` set, blocking withdraw exactly like any other trap fall. **3-6 — "find"**: the parked crypt converts to ordinary floor treasure id 21, pushed onto `state.treasures` and phase set to `pickup` for a normal, carry-capacity-gated pickup (design Resolved-13 — the crypt card itself becomes the gems). Because the code never touches `area.contents` while parked, a later `reload` of genuinely-left-behind found gems (the same numeric code, 221, once resolved) is never mistaken for a still-unopened crypt. When drawn, it fires immediately in the same hazard-resolution pass: one visible d6 (`desertionRoll{creatureId,roll,deserted,items}`) per status-1 ALLY, in roster order — original (status 0) members never roll. A Wolf ally (creature 20) is immune and is simply SKIPPED, emitting `wolfUnmoved` instead of rolling (design US-18, "The Wolf is unmoved."). A roll of 1-2 removes the ally from the game OUTRIGHT, with everything they carried — borne or merely carried alike — leaving with them rather than spilling to the floor (Bell Rope's roll-1 "vanish" is the precedent, SC-EXT-8: not dead, no `memberDied`, not revivable); `items` snapshots the ally's carried treasure ids at roll time (`[...a.treasure]`, taken before any removal) so the design's "taking [treasure list]" Feedback can be itemized by the presentation layer regardless of outcome — populated whether they leave or stay, though only the deserting case ever displays it. Deserters are collected by reference across the whole roll and filtered out of `state.party` in one pass afterward (Mutiny's own removal pattern) rather than spliced one at a time, so mid-loop splicing never shifts the roster-order indices of allies still to roll. `desertionRoll.creatureId` is a creature id, not a party index, for the same post-splice-safety reason as `bellRoll.creatureId` (deviating from the design brief's literal `memberId` field name). | SC-EXT-21 | The Demon (US-13): draw-classify relocation into `prev`, a forced no-reaction-test ambush on (re-)entry or withdrawal from EVERY entry path (chamber, plain tunnel/Gateway, AND the Deep Pool/Viper Pit specials alike), a Spectre-style magic-only fight gate extended with a Magic Axe clause, and a design-verbatim kill notice. **Draw-classify** (`chamber.ts`'s `classify`, intercepting creature code `100+15` before even the Gallery-petrify check — closing the seam Task 7 left open, where a Gallery-drawn Demon used to arrive as a plain exempt stranger; `GALLERY_EXEMPT_CREATURES` no longer lists it): a fresh Demon draw never enters `state.strangers` or `state.statues` — `spawnDemon` parks `100+15` onto `state.areas[state.prev]`'s `contents` instead (`state.prev` is always a valid index from `newGame` onward, so the design's "materialize in the current area" fallback for a missing `prev` is defensive only, never reached in play), emitting `demonSpawned`; if that `prev` is earthquake-collapsed (`AF_DESTROYED`), it disperses instead — no state change beyond `demonDispersed`. Threaded through both small-pack draw paths: `enterChamber`'s first-visit draw and `drawSmallCards` (the Well/Bell extra-draw, SC-EXT-7/SC-EXT-8), both now accepting the caller's `events` array so a Demon drawn either way still relocates. **Ambush** (`reduce.ts`'s `ambushIfDemon`, forcing combat outright — no `encounter` phase, no reaction test — whenever `state.strangers` contains the Demon, emitting `demonUnfolds`): a shared `pullParkedDemon(state, area)` helper first pulls a parked `100+15` marker off any area's persisted `contents` into the LIVE `state.strangers`, for every entry path that doesn't already run `enterChamber`'s own reload cycle — the tunnel/Gateway branch of `resolveAreaLoop`; the Deep Pool AND Viper Pit special branches (both return from `resolveAreaLoop` BEFORE the generic tunnel branch, and BEFORE their own treasure-reclaim/telegraph logic, so each calls `pullParkedDemon` at the very top of its own branch — entry-ambush fires first, ahead of any of the special's own logic, which only matters once the party has survived arriving; the pool's own CROSSING effects are a separate code path that only fires when the party later LEAVES by a fresh doorway, so this ordering is coherent); and the `withdraw` case, pulling a marker off the DESTINATION area's contents before checking. A revisited area with no parked Demon (e.g. one already killed there) behaves exactly as it did pre-SC-EXT-21. Review fix: `finishChamber` calls `ambushIfDemon` FIRST, before its own `pacifiedAreas` re-park branch (`settlePacifiedArea`) — a Demon is always hostile (never "pacified by indifference"), so one parked into an area the party had already permanently pacified (an earlier draw made elsewhere while they were away, same as `withdraw` above) must still ambush on ordinary re-entry instead of being silently swallowed back into `settlePacifiedArea`'s re-park (or, with a Thief present, slipped into a live pickup) forever. This hoist is a plain reordering with no new branch: `ambushIfDemon` already no-ops (returns `false`) whenever no Demon is present, so every non-Demon `pacifiedAreas` re-entry — and, since a base `AREA_CARDS`/kit-off game can never contain creature 15 at all, EVERY kit-off game — passes straight through unaffected (byte-identical, SC-EXT-1). **Fight gate** (`combatPlan.ts`): the Spectre-only magic-only machinery (`engageable`, the per-match `spectreNeedsMagic` validation, the engage-all `canFightWithFree` check, `previewPlan`'s strongest-combination exclusion and magic-vs-strength `memberStr` selection, and `resolvePlannedRound`'s idle-and-unfightable auto-slay) is generalized from a single `C_SPECTRE` check to a `MAGIC_ONLY_IDS = [C_SPECTRE, C_DEMON]` set, renaming `isSpectreIdx`→`isMagicOnlyIdx` and `spectreMatch`→`magicOnlyMatch` throughout; a new `magicOnlyBypass(state,m,sid)` dispatches to the existing `canSwordSpectre` for a Spectre or a new `canAxeDemon` for a Demon — Magic Axe (treasure 17) possession alone qualifies ANY creature (no species restriction, unlike the Sword-Spectre precedent's `[0,1,5,6]` list; full borne/strength-bonus wiring is a later kit task, US-24). An ordinary fighter set against a Demon is rejected with the new `demonNeedsMagic` `PlanError` (parallel to `spectreNeedsMagic`, kept distinct so the UI's raw `planRejected` text stays accurate). An idle, unfightable Demon triggers the SAME strongest-member auto-slay `resolvePlannedRound` already gives an idle Spectre (Resolved-6), emitting the new `demonSlew{creatureId}` (the victim) rather than `spectreSlew`, purely so the presentation layer's flavour text can name the right monster; only one slaying fires per round even if an idle Spectre AND an idle Demon are simultaneously unfightable (an untested, vanishingly rare combination). **Kill notice** (`apps/web/src/game/eventNotices.ts`): the base `strangerKilled{creatureId}` event is generic and normally silent at the notice layer (folded into the fight overlay's own "N foe(s) down" line) — a `creatureId===15` branch gives the Demon its own design-verbatim line instead (US-13 Feedback: "The Demon collapses into ash."), leaving every ordinary foe's kill silent exactly as before. | chamber.ts:14-24,49-105,214-227, reduce.ts:33,159-181,302-309,375-380,396-398,415-419,660-664, combatPlan.ts:16-18,22,27,37-43,60-71,96-136,158-247,357-377, actions.ts:209-222, apps/web/src/game/eventNotices.ts:106-111 | kit-apprentice-demon.test.ts › appears in prev's contents, not the chamber; › disperses instead when prev was collapsed by an Earthquake; › relocates even out of a Gallery draw — closing Task 7's plain-exemption seam; › forces a hostile fight on entry into a CHAMBER holding a parked Demon — no reaction test; › forces the SAME ambush entering a plain TUNNEL holding a parked Demon; › forces the SAME ambush entering a Deep Pool tile holding a parked Demon (fix round); › forces the SAME ambush entering a Viper Pit tile holding a parked Demon (fix round); › the Deep Pool behaves normally again once the Demon there is gone (fix round); › forces the ambush when the party WITHDRAWS back into a Demon-holding area; › forces the fight instead of settlePacifiedArea silently re-parking it (review fix); › pacified re-park behaves exactly as before when there's no Demon to ambush (review fix); › rejects an ordinary fighter set against it; › accepts a caster or a Magic Axe bearer (any species) against it; The peek fires only when SOME surviving (status 0/1) member carries the Idol (`idolCarried`); a copy resting on a dead (status 3) or stone (status 2) member alone, or left on the chamber floor, never rolls at all and scores exactly 0, the same as it would for a fixed-value treasure a non-counting member holds. | SC-EXT-27 | The Magic Shield (US-23, artifact 20, Resolved-15): `BORNEABLE` gains 20 (SC-7.3-13) — like the Axe, petrifies/lost with a stoned or fallen bearer. Unlike every other borneable artefact, its passive effect is NOT possession-wide: `shieldWardActive(state,member)` (effects.ts) is TRUE only for a LIVING, eligible bearer — Hero(0)/W-Hero(1)/Man(5)/Woman(6), the Sword's own class list (Resolved-9) — holding it, with no active Eye of God; "any member may pick up, carry, or receive the Shield" (`setBorne` has no creature-type gate of its own, SC-7.3-13) but the ward is simply INERT for an ineligible holder (a Wizard, say) — bearable, harmless. When active, the ward is **pairing-scoped** (design's central new mechanic): `combatPlan.ts`'s `matchShielded(state, front)` checks ONLY a single match's own front line — never the whole party — so `previewPlan`'s per-stranger mp computation (`strangerMP`) nullifies (an ordinary foe, mp→0) or, for the Sorcerer(11)/Apprentice(14), weakens by an extra −2 (`shieldedMP`, stacking additively on top of whatever Lotus Dust/Holy Water/the Eye already subtracted via `enemyMP`, floored at 0 by the SAME `Math.max(0,…)` those already use) ONLY the stranger(s) in THAT match — a stranger paired against a different match's front, or a leftover enemy caster folded in from the background (`enemyBackers`, §395 — not literally "paired" against anyone), is untouched. This is baked directly into `enemyStr`, so the fight UI's preview and the actual roll (`resolvePlannedRound`, which calls `previewPlan` internally) always agree; `previewPlan` also surfaces it as an enemy-side `"Magic Shield · <name>"` modifier chip, its value recomputed from `enemyMP` (not a flat −2) so it always matches the ACTUAL, possibly-floored change. One `shieldWarded{creatureId,mode}` event fires per stranger the ward ACTUALLY reduced (base mp>0) in a resolved round — `mode` "nullify" or "weaken" — never merely because the Shield is in play; the presentation layer renders the design's own verbatim per-mode text ("The Magic Shield turns the [creature]'s power aside." / "The Shield dims the [Sorcerer]'s power (−2).", apps/web/src/game/eventNotices.ts). An active Eye of God nullifies the ward outright (`shieldWardActive`'s own `!eyeActive` gate) — distinct from, and on top of, the Eye's OWN separate −2 against the Sorcerer (SC-9.4-8): with the Eye active the Shield contributes nothing further. | SC-EXT-28 | Spell (US-22) is hazard id 8, appended last after Quarrel; it always fires (no park/fizzle pre-check skips the generic `hazardFired`, unlike Harpies/Quarrel — Trap/Mutiny's precedent). It is eligible only when `prev` is a distinct (`prev!==partyArea`), un-destroyed (`!(flags&AF_DESTROYED)`), non-gateway (`special!==SPECIAL_GATEWAY`) TUNNEL (`!dec.chamber`) and the large pack still has a card to draw; otherwise it fizzles, emitting `spellRemap{fizzled:true}` with NO state change. On success: `prev`'s raw card value splices into the middle of the REMAINING (undrawn) large pack — `largeIdx + floor(remaining/2)` — so it can turn up again as a later draw; the map cell at `prev`'s coordinate is then replaced by a brand-new `PlacedArea` holding the NEXT card off the pack (`visited:false`, empty `contents`, no `mirroredStairs`/`secretDoor` — the old tile's history is gone per design), flagged `AF_UNRESOLVED` (state.ts) so the renderer can show it face-down; emits `spellRemap{fizzled:false}`. `AF_UNRESOLVED` is cleared the moment the party's `resolveArea` (reduce.ts) next lands there — covering `move`/carpet/retreat/`relocateDown` alike, which is also where the replaced area's real first resolution (a chamber draw if it turns out to be a chamber, or a plain tunnel; mirrored-stair treatment via the ordinary `tryMove` logic if entered vertically) actually happens, unaided by any special-case code. | SC-EXT-31 | Cave-global Apprentice revert on an MP Sorcerer kill, plus the remaining MP-side kit-id lookup sweep (M8, extension-kit multiplayer milestone task 2, design US-14): there is only ONE Sorcerer in a multiplayer cave, so the instant ANY seat kills him — a union command or a lone party via ordinary chamber combat, UNION-AGNOSTIC by design (mirroring `multi-zombies.ts`'s `zombiePostSweep`, whose Sorcerer-death annihilation already fires off any seat's `sorcererKilled` with no union gating, SC-MP-37) — `unionPostAction` runs a standalone step (3b, ahead of and independent from the union-bounty-share step) that reuses the solo revert (`revertApprenticesOnSorcererDeath`, effects.ts, SC-EXT-20) across **every seat's `PartyState`**, whether or not the killer or the reverting seat is in any union — an Apprentice ally reverts to a hostile stranger in whichever seat's party she currently occupies (cast `PartyState` to `GameState`, the same idiom `compose()` uses; the function only touches `party`/`strangers`/`treasures`, all present on `PartyState` verbatim). WHERE she and her spilled items land is decided per seat, by whether that seat has an OPEN chamber session. The ACTING seat, and any bystander already mid-`encounter`/`fight`/`medusa`/`pickup` (`LIVE_WORKING_SET`), take the solo placement unchanged — into that seat's LIVE working set — which is coherent precisely because such a session ends in `persistAndExplore` parking the set back onto its own tile. A BYSTANDER AT REST (`phase: "explore"`, and likewise a terminal seat) has no such session: its working set is empty by invariant, nothing would ever park it, and its next `enterChamber` RESETS `strangers`/`treasures` (chamber.ts:115-117) — a working-set placement there would mean the promised hostile stranger NEVER materializes and her carried items are deleted from the game (an item-conservation violation). For those seats the revert is written straight onto the SHARED tile the seat stands on — `cave.areas[partyArea].contents`, as `100+C_APPRENTICE` and `200+<item id>` — the same cave-shared channel a disagreed recruit at dissolution, step 3's guarded loot and the zombies variant's working-set park all use (SC-EXT-32), so she waits there for whichever seat next enters, that seat's own working set untouched. The killing seat's own composed party was already reverted inside the solo reduce this hook runs after, so calling it again there is a safe no-op (nothing left matching `status===1 && creatureId===C_APPRENTICE`). A LOANED Apprentice who reverts vanishes from her commander's array along with her `mpTag`, so EVERY active union (not only one the killer commands) is re-indexed (`reindexUnion`) immediately afterward — the loan ends cleanly, with no dangling `onLoan` record, exactly like a mutiny-desertion (SC-MP-32). The pre-existing union-bounty-share step (I-19, SC-MP-33) is unaffected: it still stamps `sorcererKilled`/`sorcererSharedWith` only for a genuine union kill (≥2 members) — a separate concern from this row's cave-global revert. Separately, `multi-fight.ts`'s `pvpView` swept its last base-only lookup: `nameOf` now resolves creature names via `ALL_CREATURES` (SC-EXT-2) instead of `CREATURES`, so a kit creature (e.g. Witch, id 18) fighting in PvP no longer crashes the per-engagement name preview. Zero base-only `CREATURES[`/`TREASURES[`/`HAZARD_NAMES[` indexing remains anywhere in `packages/engine/src/multi*.ts`. | multi-union.ts:396-404,458-500, multi-fight.ts:718-720 | multi-union.test.ts › cave-global Apprentice revert on an MP Sorcerer kill (SC-EXT-31, design US-14) › reverts another seat's Apprentice ally on a NON-union solo kill (no union ever formed); › reverts an Apprentice ally in the KILLING (commander) seat's own party; › reverts an Apprentice ally in ANOTHER, uninvolved seat's party — the Sorcerer is cave-global; › a LOANED Apprentice ends her loan cleanly when she reverts, and her item spills; › parks a BYSTANDER seat's reverted Apprentice on her tile, where the next chamber entry cannot erase her; › two seats each holding an Apprentice both revert at one kill — the acting seat live, the bystander parked; › a bystander already mid-encounter keeps the live working-set semantics — her chamber is still open; multi-kit.test.ts › PvP kit-creature lookups (SC-EXT-31) — no base-only CREATURES[]/TREASURES[] indexing › a PvP fight where a kit creature (Witch, id 18) fights does not crash and previews her name |

and `mpReduceInner` resyncs `cave.cryptCoord` from the acting seat's own composed result after every action, propagating a fresh park (`classify`) or a spend (`enterCrypt`) to every other seat's next composed view — "the first seat to act [to call `enterCrypt`] takes the risk/reward." | multi.ts:61-73,196-198,509-514 (the Crypt fix;
